✻ Conversation compacted (ctrl+o for history)


⏺ The data settles it, and not in favor of the thing I built.

  0A. Premise challenge: is research solving a real problem?

  Five research runs across your five targets. Two were rejected outright
  (confirmed: false) — a 40% rejection rate. One of those was the Palantir round
  where research confidently contradicted your HackerRank preview, and you were
  right, it was wrong. You also have three Amazon targets, which is re-run
  friction showing up in the data.

  The damning part is the commit log. Three of my last eight commits exist
  solely to defend against this feature:

  33a5f86  evidence hierarchy: the candidate's eyes beat base rates
  cf2a3b4  intake policy: include, never adjudicate
  6d57131  stop discarding citations we already retrieved

  A feature whose primary engineering artifact is defenses against itself is a 
  feature arguing to be cut. I wrote all three believing I was hardening intake.
  I was building sandbags around a leak.

  And there's a harm worse than being wrong: research manufactures false 
  confidence. Eleven citations saying "coding + SQL + API" reads as
  authoritative and displaces what the student actually knows. That's strictly
  worse than silence for the exact user we're serving — someone anxious and
  looking for something to trust.

  0B. What already does the job

  You named the one case that worked: pasting your OA problem titles, and the 
  model correctly reading the last stage as optimization-themed. Notice what
  that is. You supplied primary evidence about your instance; the model
  interpreted it. Same with Erik's WhatsApp notes, which produced two correct
  drafts with the async emphasis.

  That's the line, and it's clean:

  ┌──────────────────────────┬──────────────────────────────────────────────┐
  │           Mode           │              Track record here               │
  ├──────────────────────────┼──────────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ Interpreting what the    │ Erik's notes → 2 correct drafts. OA titles → │
  │ user has                 │  correct theme. 2 for 2.                     │
  ├──────────────────────────┼──────────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ Retrieving base rates    │ Palantir → wrong. Amazon → stale premise. 0  │
  │ from the web             │ for 5 useful, 2 rejected.                    │
  └──────────────────────────┴──────────────────────────────────────────────┘

  The model's priors already contain the generic shapes ("Amazon OA is two
  coding problems"). Search doesn't add that — it adds specificity, which is
  precisely what goes wrong.

  0C. The gap this exposes

  Your Cowork workflow had three steps. We built two:

  1. Make a plan from what you know          ✓ built (intake + clarifier)
  2. ADAPT the plan as you learn new things   ✗ NEVER BUILT
  3. Practice with AI as interviewer/generator ✓ built (session + judge)

  Preemptive research was a bad substitute for step 2. It tried to front-load
  knowledge you don't have yet instead of letting knowledge arrive when it
  actually arrives — which is how prep really works. You get the invite email
  Tuesday, a friend replies Thursday, you see the assessment preview Sunday.

  Today a plan is frozen at intake. There's no "I just learned this" path.
  That's the missing capability, and it's worth more than research ever was.

  Mode: SCOPE REDUCTION. You proposed a cut, the evidence supports it, and I'm
  not going to argue for keeping my own code.

⏺ User answered Claude's questions:
  ⎿  · D1 — How far do we cut research, and what replaces it?

     Project/branch: interview_prep on main; 5 research runs, 2 rejected 
     outright, 3 defensive commits.

     ELI10: We agree research shouldn't be on the critical path. The open 
     question is whether to delete it or demote it, and whether to spend the 
     freed effort building the step your Cowork workflow had that we never 
     shipped — dropping new information onto an existing plan and having it 
     re-shape. That adaptation step is what research was badly substituting for.

     Stakes if we pick wrong: delete too eagerly and a student with no network

     plans stay frozen at the moment you knew least.

     Recommendation: A — it removes research from every default path (which is 
     the actual defect), keeps the capability for the networkless student behind
     an explicit ask, and spends the freed effort on the step you actually 
     used. → Demote + build adaptation

  ---
  Section 1: Architecture Review

  Scope under review: research leaves the default path (survives as opt-in); new
  "add what you learned" adaptation path.

  Dependency graph — before / after

  BEFORE (research on the critical edge)

    client ──▶ /api/target ──▶ intake.ts ──▶ targets/<id>/target.json
           ──▶ /api/research ──────────────▶ research.ts ──▶ claude -p
  (WebSearch)   ◀── 2-4 min, BLOCKING
           ──▶ /api/research/confirm ──────▶ t.research.confirmed = true
           ──▶ /api/clarify ──────────────▶ clarify.ts ──▶ Anthropic API
           ──▶ /api/accept-spec ──────────▶ validateRoundSpec ──▶ proposeQueue
  ──▶ plan-topics
           ──▶ /api/generate ─────────────▶ spawnGeneration

  AFTER

    client ──▶ /api/target ──▶ /api/clarify ──▶ /api/accept-spec ──▶
  /api/generate     ◀── ~15s to a plan
           ┊
           ┊ (opt-in, off the spine)      ┊ (new, post-plan)
           └▶ /api/research ─┐            └▶ /api/learn ──▶ ??? ──▶ queue
  mutation
                             └▶ t.research.confirmed ──▶ read by clarify +
  plan-topics (UNCHANGED)

  Removing research from the spine is architecturally clean: t.research is
  already an optional field, /api/clarify:560 and /api/accept-spec:650 both
  guard on ?.confirmed, and research.ts is dynamically imported. Nothing
  downstream needs to change. OK.

  The new edge is where the problem is.

  The finding: there is no re-plan path, and the queue is single-assignment

  app.ts:636 — if (!loadQueue(repoRoot, t.id)) — the queue is built exactly once
  and never rebuilt. QueueItem has no stale state, and by the time new
  information arrives, items are spread across five statuses with materialized 
  artifacts on disk:

    pending ──▶ generating ──▶ ready ──▶ done
       │            │            │
       │            └─▶ failed   └─▶ problems/<item-id>/  ◀── a real generated
  problem exists
       └─▶ skipped

    "Erik says it's async-heavy" arrives HERE. What moves?

  pending is safely re-shapeable. done is history and must not move. ready is
  the hard case — a problem is already written to targets/<id>/problems/<item>/
  and the user may open it tomorrow expecting the shape the plan promised.

  CRITICAL — this is the load-bearing decision in the accepted scope. Everything
  else in the adaptation feature follows from it.

⏺ User answered Claude's questions:
  ⎿  · D2 — When new information arrives, what actually changes in an existing 
     plan?

     Project/branch: interview_prep on main. `app.ts:636` builds the queue once 
     and never rebuilds it; `QueueItem` has no stale state.

     ELI10: You made a plan on Monday. On Thursday a friend tells you the round 
     is async-heavy, not SQL. Some rounds you've already done, one problem is 
     already written and sitting on disk waiting for you, the rest are just 
     names on a timeline. We have to decide which of those the new information 
     is allowed to touch.

     Stakes if we pick wrong: too aggressive and a problem you were about to 
     work on silently turns into a different problem, or finished work vanishes 
     from your history; too timid and the feature doesn't actually adapt 
     anything and you're back to remaking the plan by hand — which is the Cowork
     gap we're trying to close.

     Recommendation: A — it's the only option where nothing you've already 
     touched moves under you, and it still re-shapes everything that hasn't been
     built yet, which is the majority of a season plan. → Pending only, ready 
     is offered

⏺ D2 = A. Pending re-shapes freely; ready gets flagged and offered a rebuild;
  done/generating are frozen.

  ---
  Section 2: Error & Rescue Map

  REDUCTION mode — critical paths only. The new path is /api/learn.

    METHOD/CODEPATH           | WHAT CAN GO WRONG              | EXCEPTION CLASS

  --------------------------|--------------------------------|------------------
    POST /api/learn           | target_id missing/unknown      | (404 by
  pattern)
                              | material empty after trim      | (400 by
  pattern)
                              | queue.json absent (no plan yet)| RecordNotFound
    re-clarify call           | model refuses / malformed      | gateClarify
  throw
                              | max_tokens truncation          | Anthropic
  APIError
                              | every draft fails the gate     | Error
  (per-draft leniency)
    queue mutation            | zero pending items to re-shape | (no-op, valid)
                              | spec_id references dropped spec| dangling
  reference
    plan-topics re-naming     | gate rejects titles            | Error
  (degrades: quiet rows)
    disk write                | crash between the two saves    | ← GAP

    EXCEPTION CLASS           | RESCUED? | RESCUE ACTION            | USER SEES
    --------------------------|----------|--------------------------|-----------
  ---------
    RecordNotFound            | Y        | 404                      | "no plan
  to update"
    gateClarify throw         | Y        | fall back to infer (:574) | plain
  re-inference
    Anthropic APIError        | Y        | 502 + material preserved | "couldn't
  re-plan — try again"
    topics gate reject        | Y        | per-spec catch (:654)    | quiet rows
  (existing)
    dangling spec_id          | N ← GAP  | —                        | timeline
  row with no spec
    crash mid-adapt           | N ← GAP  | —                        | SILENT
  mismatch ← BAD

  Two gaps, and they're the same gap wearing different clothes: an adapt is a 
  multi-file mutation with no record that it happened. saveTarget writes
  target.json, saveQueue writes queue.json. accept-spec survives a crash between
  them because if (!loadQueue) rebuilds — but adapt has no such self-heal,
  because the queue already exists. The user is left with a plan that quietly
  disagrees with its own specs.

  The same missing artifact causes a product problem: a plan that changes with 
  no explanation is spooky. If rounds 4-6 renamed themselves overnight, the user
  needs to know what caused it.

  One record fixes both.

⏺ User answered Claude's questions:
  ⎿  · D3 — Does an adapt leave a record on disk?

     Project/branch: interview_prep on main. `saveTarget` and `saveQueue` are 
     separate writes (`app.ts:635`, `:658`) with no transaction and no self-heal
     once a queue exists.

     ELI10: When new information re-shapes your plan, two files on disk have to 
     change together. If something dies in between, they disagree and nothing 
     tells you. Separately — even when it works perfectly — rounds silently 
     renaming themselves is unnerving unless you can see what caused it. A 
     single log entry per adapt solves both: it explains the change to you, and 
     it lets the app notice when a change only half-landed.

     Stakes if we pick wrong: without a record, a crashed adapt leaves you 
     practicing against a plan that doesn't match its own specs with no symptom;
     and every successful adapt looks like the app changing its mind for no 
     reason, which is exactly the untrustworthy feeling that got research cut.

     Recommendation: A — one small append-only array is the cheapest thing that 
     makes adaptation both auditable and recoverable, and it's the artifact the 
     timeline needs anyway to explain itself. → Adapt log on the target

  Section 4: Data Flow & Interaction Edge Cases

  Searched for 1 pattern (ctrl+o to expand)

⏺   material ──▶ VALIDATE ──▶ RE-CLARIFY ──▶ MUTATE (memory) ──▶ VALIDATE ──▶
  PERSIST ──▶ RENDER
        │           │             │               │                  │
   │          │
        ▼           ▼             ▼               ▼                  ▼
   ▼          ▼
    [empty?]   [>256KB?]    [model fail?]   [0 pending?]      [dangling
  [queue,      [poll
    [nil?]     [not text?]  [gate fail?]    [all done?]        spec_id?]
  then target]  stomp?]

    INTERACTION             | EDGE CASE                          | HANDLED? |
  HOW
    ------------------------|------------------------------------|----------|---
  ------------------------
    Paste "what I learned"  | poll fires while typing            | Y        |
  userIsTyping() (app.js:434)
                            | double-click submit                | N ← GAP  | no
  in-flight guard
                            | navigate away mid-adapt            | Y        |
  server completes; re-render
    Adapt in flight (~60s)  | generate clicked on an item        | N ← CRIT |
  load-await-save race
                            |   the adapt is re-shaping          |          |
                            | second adapt submitted             | N ← GAP  |
  two concurrent re-plans
    Result                  | zero pending items (all done)      | N ← GAP  |
  must say so, not silent-succeed
                            | re-clarify drops a spec in use     | N ← GAP  |
  dangling spec_id on timeline
    Flagged ready item      | user ignores flag and launches     | Y        |
  intentional per D2-A

  The double-click, no-op, and dangling-spec_id gaps are mechanical — in-flight
  button disable, an explicit "nothing left to re-shape" message, and never
  dropping a spec that has non-done items. Those go straight to tasks.

  CRITICAL GAP is the race, and it's a shape this repo has already been bitten
  by twice — the /api/research load-await-save race (app.ts:595-599) and the
  session port collision. /api/learn loads the queue, awaits a model for ~60s,
  then writes. If the user clicks generate on item 7 in that window, the adapt's
  stale snapshot reverts item 7 to pending while a generation subprocess is
  genuinely running — an orphan, exactly what sweepOrphanedGenerations() exists
  to clean up after.

  Re-loading before save isn't sufficient here, because the adapt's decision
  about which items to re-shape was made against the stale snapshot.
⏺ User answered Claude's questions:
  ⎿  · D4 — How does an in-flight adapt avoid colliding with generation?

     Project/branch: interview_prep on main. Same bug shape as the 
     `/api/research` race already fixed at `app.ts:595`; `/api/generate` already
     refuses concurrency with a 409 at `:671`.

     ELI10: Re-planning takes about a minute because it calls a model. If you 
     hit "generate" on a round during that minute, the re-plan finishes holding 
     a stale picture of your queue and writes it back — undoing the generate 
     while the generation is actually still running in the background. You end 
     up with an orphaned process and a row that lies about its own state.

     Stakes if we pick wrong: a silently orphaned generation is the failure mode
     that already cost a full practice session earlier in this project, when a 
     port collision meant the round you practiced wasn't the round the app 
     thought you practiced. Same class of bug, same silence.

     Recommendation: C — the lock prevents the collision in every normal case, 
     and the re-load makes the outcome correct even when the lock can't help, 
     like a server restart mid-adapt. Neither alone covers both. → Both lock and
     re-load (Recommended)

  Sections 5–10

  §5 Code Quality — 3 findings, all decided by existing repo precedent:
  - DRY: /api/learn would duplicate the findings-assembly + fallback-to-infer
  block at app.ts:559-583. Extract clarifyOrInfer(t, answers) and call it from
  both.
  - Complexity: the handler would branch 8+ ways. Every other module here
  (queue.ts, clarify.ts, validate.ts) is a pure tested function with app.ts as
  thin wiring. New server/src/adapt.ts exporting a pure planAdaptation(target, 
  queue, newSpecs) → AdaptDiff; handler stays wiring.
  - Deliberate debt: research.ts + 11 tests + prompts/research-round.md stay
  maintained for a path that may go unpressed. That's the cost you accepted in
  D1; research.ts's header comment should record why it was demoted.

  §6 Tests — no decisions, one clear gap. adapt.test.ts mirroring queue.test.ts:
  pure, no model calls (repo convention), now passed in like bucketIntoDays
  already does. The 2am-Friday test: adapt while an item is generating → assert 
  the generating item is untouched and the skip is recorded in the log.
  Hostile-QA: adapt with every item done; adapt that would drop a spec still
  referenced by a ready item; two adapts at once. Chaos: kill between the queue
  and target writes → assert reconcile-on-load flags it. No flakiness risk if
  adapt.ts stays pure.

  §7 Performance — no issues, a straight win. Default intake drops a 2–4 minute
  blocking subprocess: ~2min → ~15s. Adapt's ~60s call is user-initiated and off
  the critical path. Adapt log is bounded in practice (<20/season).

  §8 Observability — no gaps remaining. D3's adapt log answers "why did my plan
  change" for the user; reconcile-on-load makes a half-applied adapt detectable
  rather than silent; server-side console.log follows the existing [app] …
  style.

  §9 Deploy — 1 risk, mitigated. No DB, no migrations. Additive optional fields
  load cleanly against existing targets (verified above). Rollback is git revert
  — research code stays in tree, so reversing the cut is a client rewire.
  WARNING (pre-existing, out of scope): saveTarget/saveQueue use bare
  writeFileSync with no temp-then-rename, so a crash mid-write truncates the
  file and no loader validates. → TODO.

  §10 Trajectory — Reversibility 4/5. Adaptation is additive; the research cut
  is reversible. Debt: one opt-in path kept warm. The 1-year question passes
  only if the why is written down — a future reader will otherwise see working
  research code sitting off the main path and re-promote it.

  §11 Design — skipped per SCOPE REDUCTION mode. One consequence you should
  know: the three findings-screen mockups from the interrupted
  /plan-design-review are now moot. They existed to make an 11-row citation wall
  tolerable for everyone; with research opt-in, the only person who sees that
  wall explicitly asked for sources and wants to read them. That review's D2
  decision ("clarify before research") partially survives — it collapses into
  plain clarify-first, which is now just the flow.

⏺ CODEX SAYS (plan review — outside voice):
  ════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════
  Problems the review still seems to miss:

  - The simplest version is probably manual spec editing plus "regenerate
  pending queue," not an LLM-powered adaptation system with locks, stale flags,
  logs, recovery semantics, and diff planning. For a single-user September tool,
  this may be overbuilt.
  - "Scope reduction" is muddy. Research stays in tree, endpoints stay, tests
  stay, confirm UI stays, and now a second adaptive planning feature is added.
  This reduces default-path latency, but not maintenance surface.
  - Mutating RoundSpecs is dangerous unless specs are versioned. Existing
  ready/done problems may have been generated from an old spec shape. If the
  target spec changes in place, history becomes misleading. You probably need
  immutable spec revisions or per-item spec snapshots.
  - "Re-clarify must never drop a spec still referenced by a non-done item" is
  under-specified. What about done items? If a done item references a dropped
  spec, the timeline/reporting layer still needs that spec to explain what
  happened.
  - Writing queue.json first, then target.json, is not obviously safe. If queue
  items now reference new spec IDs that only exist in the not-yet-written
  target, the intermediate state is invalid. Calling the target log entry the
  "commit marker" does not solve readers seeing the half-state.
  - A persistent adapting flag can brick the target after a crash unless there
  is explicit recovery, TTL, owner PID, or lock cleanup behavior. The plan
  mentions detectability but not the actual load-time repair rule.
  - "Disk-derived lock" is doing too much hand-waving. Is it atomic
  mkdir/open(O_EXCL)? Per target or global? What happens on stale locks, process
  death, Windows/macOS differences, or two server processes?
  - "Reload-before-write" is not concurrency control unless it compares a
  version/hash/mtime from the snapshot used to compute the diff. Otherwise it
  only narrows the race.
  - The adaptation diff depends on "newSpecs," but the hard part is how those
  are produced. Reusing clarifier may not be enough because clarifier is built
  for intake, not reconciliation against existing specs/items/history.
  - "Pending items reshape freely" may still be bad UX if the user has seen
  those items in the queue. Silent renaming/reassignment can make the plan feel
  unstable. You need some visible before/after diff or confirmation.
  - "Ready item whose shape no longer matches" assumes you can determine
  mismatch reliably. If this is based only on spec reassignment, okay. If based
  on semantic fit, that is another model judgment with false positives.
  - Rebuild semantics are missing. Does rebuilding overwrite the problem
  directory, create a new item, archive the old one, or preserve both?
  Overwriting destroys useful context and makes stale flags harder to audit.
  - items_reshaped[] and items_flagged[] are probably insufficient. You need
  old/new spec IDs, old/new names, reason, and skipped items, or the log will
  not support debugging/recovery.
  - Storing material_excerpt is a privacy footgun. Invite emails and friend
  messages may contain names, emails, links, phone numbers, and company-specific
  details. The plan caps size but not sensitivity.
  - "Pasted links are stored as text and never fetched" avoids SSRF, but the LLM
  may still treat malicious pasted content as instructions. The prompt needs
  explicit source-boundary handling.
  - Keeping /api/research reachable from the intake form means the product still
  needs to support long blocking calls, cancellation, retries, progress/error
  states, and stale results. The default path avoids it; the codebase does not.
  - The current lack of schema validation becomes worse with adaptations. You
  are adding more states and recovery logic on top of unchecked JSON.parse as T.
  Deferring validation while adding state transitions is a bad trade.
  - The queue status enum grows informally with adapting. Is that a queue-level
  state or item-level status? If it is stuffed into queue.json, every loader/UI
  path must understand it.
  - /api/generate refusing during adapt is fine, but what about
  /api/accept-spec, /api/clarify, research confirm, or another browser tab with
  stale target state? The plan only names generate/adapt.
  - The one-at-a-time generation constraint may conflict with adaptation UX. If
  a generation takes minutes, the user cannot paste new information while
  waiting. That may be acceptable, but it should be explicit.
  - The plan assumes the existing plan-topics LLM call can rename reshaped
  pending items correctly. If names are used as stable user-facing anchors,
  renaming can break recognition.
  - There is no rollback story. If adaptation produces a worse plan, the user
  should be able to reject the proposed diff before write, or undo the last
  adaptation.
  - "Adapt appends log after computing validated full state" still means bad
  model output can permanently alter the target unless there is a
  preview/confirm step. The user's whole lesson from research was that model
  confidence is dangerous.
  - The strategic risk: the real winning behavior was interpreting primary
  material. That may call for a "paste artifact -> extract facts -> show
  inferred changes for approval" workflow, not autonomous reshaping.
  - The pricing/future product angle argues against complex local-disk
  transaction machinery. If this may later be paid, the no-database architecture
  and unchecked JSON persistence are likely the wrong foundation to keep
  deepening.
  ════════════════════════════════════════════════════════════

  It landed a hit I missed, and it's the one that hurts.

  CROSS-MODEL TENSION:
    Confirm gate: My review designed adaptation as autonomous — model re-shapes,
    log explains afterward. Codex says that repeats research's exact mistake:
    research was confirm-gated and STILL got cut for being confidently wrong;
    adaptation is not gated at all and writes directly to your plan.
    I find Codex more compelling. I built the after-the-fact explanation (D3)
    instead of the before-the-fact veto, having just spent this whole review
    arguing that unverifiable model confidence is the defect.

  CROSS-MODEL TENSION:
    Spec mutation: My review assumed specs mutate in place. Codex notes `done`
    items reference those specs — mutating them makes your practice history lie
    about what you actually practiced under. It's the same class as the port
    collision: the record disagrees with what happened.

  Two others resolve with facts Codex didn't have: rebuild semantics — a ready
  item that was never launched contains no work of yours, so regenerating in
  place is safe (done is frozen); and material_excerpt privacy — targets/ is
  gitignored, verified, so it never leaves the machine. Its "scope reduction is
  muddy" hit is fair but you already priced it explicitly in D1-A.

⏺ User answered Claude's questions:
  ⎿  · D5 — Cross-model disagreement: should an adapt be previewed before it 
     writes?

     The review designed adaptation to re-shape your plan and explain afterward 
     (D3's log). The outside voice argues that's the same mistake research made 
     — and worse, because research at least had a confirm wall and adaptation 
     would have none. What I might be missing: with only pending items in play 
     and a log to read, maybe an undo is cheaper than a preview.

     RECOMMENDATION: Choose A. Every model output in this codebase that touches 
     durable state is gated — research has a confirm wall, specs have a confirm 
     step, the judge has mechanical gates, `gateTopics` degrades to quiet rows 
     rather than shipping bad names. Autonomous re-shaping would be the only 
     ungated writer, in the feature built specifically to replace a feature cut 
     for being wrong. It also makes D3 and D4 cheaper: if you approve a computed
     diff, there's one atomic apply and much less to race against.

     Completeness: A=10/10, B=6/10, C=7/10. → Accept — preview then apply

⏺ D5 = A. That simplifies D3/D4 considerably — the approved diff is the log
  entry, and the race window shrinks from ~60s to the apply moment.

  On the second tension I have a fact Codex didn't: assessments already store
  expectations_used, prompt_hash, and schema_version — a finished assessment is
  self-contained and doesn't read the live spec for its verdict. So the history
  damage is narrower than it argued, and there's a cheaper fix than revision
  machinery.

⏺ User answered Claude's questions:
  ⎿  · D6 — Cross-model disagreement: can adaptation edit an existing RoundSpec?

     The review assumed specs mutate in place. Codex says that makes your 
     practice history lie and wants immutable spec revisions or per-item 
     snapshots. What I might be missing: append-only specs could accumulate 
     orphans over a season in a way that clutters the plan.

     RECOMMENDATION: Choose B — a cheaper route to the same guarantee Codex 
     wants. Assessments already snapshot what they judged against, so finished 
     verdicts are safe either way; the only real exposure is a timeline row 
     describing a past round using a spec that has since changed underneath it. 
     Never editing a spec closes that without any versioning machinery, and it 
     makes Codex's "don't drop a spec a done item references" true by 
     construction — nothing is ever dropped or edited.

     Completeness: A=10/10, B=9/10, C=4/10. → Append-only specs (Recommended)

     │ interface AdaptDiff {                                                   │
     │   new_specs: RoundSpec[];               // append-only — never contains │
     │ an existing id                                                          │
     │   repointed: { item_id: string; from_spec_id: string; to_spec_id:       │
     │ string;                                                                 │
     │                old_title: string; new_title?: string }[];               │
     │   flagged: string[];                    // ready items built under a    │
     │ superseded spec                                                         │
     │   skipped: { item_id: string; reason: string }[];  // filled at apply   │
     │ time                                                                    │
     │   summary: string;                      // one sentence for the log +   │
     │ timeline line                                                           │
     │ }                                                                       │
     │                                                                         │
     │ interface AdaptRecord {                 // Target.adaptations[] entry   │
     │   at: string; material_excerpt: string; // ≤280 chars                   │
     │   summary: string; new_spec_ids: string[];                              │
     │   repointed: { item_id: string; from: string; to: string }[];           │
     │   flagged: string[]; skipped: string[];                                 │
     │ }                                                                       │
     │                                                                         │
     │ - gateAdapt(raw, existingSpecs) — mechanical gate in the gateClarify    │
     │ style: coerce                                                           │
     │ rounds array, run each through draftToSpec with per-draft leniency (one │
     │ bad draft                                                               │
     │ never sinks siblings), validate supersedes references an existing spec  │
     │ id or null,                                                             │
     │ reject a draft whose own id collides with an existing spec id           │
     │ (append-only), require                                                  │
     │ ≥1 surviving draft.                                                     │
     │ - planAdaptation(target, queue, drafts, now) → AdaptDiff — mechanical:  │
     │ active spec set = (existing specs not superseded) + new specs; re-point │
     │ pending                                                                 │
     │ items round-robin across it (same arithmetic as proposeQueue,           │
     │ queue.ts:89-96),                                                        │
     │ regenerating label; flag ready items whose spec is superseded; never    │
     │ touch                                                                   │
     │ done/generating/skipped. failed re-points like pending — it has no      │
     │ validated                                                               │
     │ problem.                                                                │
     │ - applyAdaptation(target, queue, diff, materialExcerpt, now) → {        │
     │ target, queue } —                                                       │
     │ returns new objects (deep-copy style like reconcileWithDisk), appends   │
     │ the                                                                     │
     │ AdaptRecord, sets stale: true on flagged items, drops (and records in   │
     │ skipped)                                                                │
     │ any re-point whose item is no longer pending/failed in the queue passed │
     │ in —                                                                    │
     │ the caller passes the freshly re-loaded queue.                          │
     │ - reconcileAdaptation(target, queue) → Queue | null — the D3 recovery   │
     │ rule: if the                                                            │
     │ last AdaptRecord lists a re-point the queue doesn't reflect, re-apply   │
     │ from the                                                                │
     │ record; null when consistent. Called from refreshQueue alongside        │
     │ reconcileWithDisk.                                                      │
     │                                                                         │
     │ server/src/queue.ts — QueueItem gains stale?: boolean (optional; the    │
     │ bare                                                                    │
     │ JSON.parse loaders accept it, no migration).                            │
     │                                                                         │
     │ server/src/intake.ts — Target gains adaptations?: AdaptRecord[]. Extend │
     │ the                                                                     │
     │ header diagram (intake.ts:4-10) with the second inbound path to         │
     │ target.json specs[].                                                    │
     │                                                                         │
     │ New prompts/adapt-plan.md — modeled on clarify-intake.md: the same      │
     │ capability                                                              │
     │ vocabulary section, the same evidence hierarchy (firsthand beats        │
     │ everything; the pasted                                                  │
     │ material IS firsthand), then: the target's CURRENT specs (id, label,    │
     │ shape line,                                                             │
     │ emphasis), the new material, and emission rules — one round per         │
     │ distinct shape the                                                      │
     │ material implies; set supersedes when a new shape replaces an existing  │
     │ spec's slot,                                                            │
     │ null when it's additional; when the material doesn't contradict a spec, │
     │ don't emit a                                                            │
     │ replacement for it. No questions in v1 — the preview screen is the      │
     │ check.                                                                  │
     │                                                                         │
     │ Model plumbing in adapt.ts — apiAdapter/claudePAdapter/pickAdapter      │
     │ mirroring clarify.ts:191-244 exactly (forced tool call; tool schema =   │
     │ ROUND_FIELDS + supersedes: {type: ['string','null']}; claude -p JSON    │
     │ fallback).                                                              │
     │                                                                         │
     │ Phase C — endpoints (server/src/app.ts)                                 │
     │                                                                         │
     │ - POST /api/adapt {target_id, material} → 404 no target, 400 empty      │
     │ material or                                                             │
     │ ▎ 256KB (loud), 400 no queue yet ("build a plan first"). Calls the      │
     │ adapter →                                                               │
     │ ▎ gateAdapt → planAdaptation, then names re-pointed items via the       │
     │ existing                                                                │
     │ ▎ pickTopicNamer (one call per affected spec, failure degrades to       │
     │ unchanged titles —                                                      │
     │ ▎ the quiet-rows rule) so the preview shows old → new names. Returns    │
     │ ▎ {diff, drafts} and writes nothing.                                    │
     │ - POST /api/adapt/apply {target_id, diff, material_excerpt} →           │
     │ server-side                                                             │
     │ re-proof (house rule, same as accept-spec): every new_specs entry       │
     │ through                                                                 │
     │ validateRoundSpec; every supersedes/to_spec_id must resolve. Re-load    │
     │ target                                                                  │
     │ and queue fresh, call applyAdaptation, write target.json then           │
     │ queue.json, return                                                      │
     │ the applied AdaptRecord (including what got skipped).                   │
     │ - POST /api/rebuild {target_id, item_id} → item must be ready + stale:  │
     │ wipe the problem dir, clear stale, status → generating, spawnGeneration │
     │ with the                                                                │
     │ item's (new) spec and title — the retry path (app.ts:681-696)           │
     │ generalized.                                                            │
     │ - /api/state additions: per-target adaptation: {at, summary} | null     │
     │ (latest                                                                 │
     │ record); stale flows through automatically via the item spread.         │
     │ - Fallback path: adapter/gate failure → 502 with the reason; material   │
     │ is in the request,                                                      │
     │ nothing persisted, retry is safe.                                       │
     │                                                                         │
     │ Phase D — client (server/src/client/app.js + CSS in appPage())          │
     │                                                                         │
     │ - Timeline page: an add what you learned link under the paceline →      │
     │ inline panel                                                            │
     │ (textarea + submit + cancel). Submit disables in-flight (no             │
     │ double-post), POST                                                      │
     │ /api/adapt, then render the diff preview in the same panel:             │
     │ summary line · new rounds (shape lines, reusing specShapeLine) ·        │
     │ re-pointed items as                                                     │
     │ old title → new title · flagged ready items ("built for the old shape — │
     │ will be                                                                 │
     │ offered a rebuild") · apply / discard. Apply → /api/adapt/apply →       │
     │ refresh(true);                                                          │
     │ response's skipped[] non-empty → banner explaining what didn't change   │
     │ and why.                                                                │
     │ Zero re-points and zero new specs at preview → say "nothing to change — │
     │ the plan                                                                │
     │ already matches" instead of an empty diff.                              │
     │ - renderSeason: item with stale gets a metaline "built for the old      │
     │ round shape" +                                                          │
     │ rebuild button (wired in wireTimeline like retry, → /api/rebuild).      │
     │ - Latest adaptation renders as one line under the paceline: Aug 5 —     │
     │ {summary}.                                                              │
     │ - The adapt textarea is covered by the existing userIsTyping() poll     │
     │ guard (app.js:434).                                                     │
     │                                                                         │
     │ Tests                                                                   │
     │                                                                         │
     │ New server/src/adapt.test.ts — house style of queue.test.ts (frozen     │
     │ NOW,                                                                    │
     │ builder helpers, one behavior per it):                                  │
     │ - gate: supersedes → unknown id throws; new-draft id colliding with     │
     │ existing spec throws                                                    │
     │ (append-only); one incoherent draft dropped, siblings survive.          │
     │ - plan: pending re-pointed round-robin; ready-under-superseded-spec     │
     │ flagged not moved;                                                      │
     │ done/generating byte-identical before/after; failed re-points like      │
     │ pending.                                                                │
     │ - apply: item that moved pending→generating between plan and apply      │
     │ lands in skipped;                                                       │
     │ original spec objects never mutated (deep-equal snapshot); excerpt      │
     │ truncated at 280.                                                       │
     │ - reconcile: record says item-3 → spec-B but queue says spec-A →        │

     │ state → null.                                                           │

     │ skip recorded.                                                          │
     │                                                                         │

     │ pattern                                                                 │

     │ line; the                                                               │
     │ re-pointed rounds carry new titles; done rows unchanged.                │
     │   - Race drill: start a generation, immediately submit an adapt         │
     │ including that item →                                                   │

⏺ User answered Claude's questions:
  ⎿  · D9 — Revising D1 for launch: opt-in, dormant, or gone?

     ELI10: We decided earlier to keep research behind a "look it up" button, 
     thinking of a student with no contacts. But your clarifier already covers 
     that student with model priors plus questions — what research added was 
     specificity, which is what it got wrong 40% of the time. For a paid launch,
     the question is whether a known-unreliable feature belongs anywhere a 
     customer can press it.

     Stakes: keep it visible and its wrongness becomes the product's wrongness 
     in front of paying users; delete it and you commit to the "interpret what 
     you bring" story fully — which is where every proven win came from anyway.

     Recommendation: A — from a launch perspective the opt-in path is a support 
     liability with no proven upside, the bootstrap case is already served by 
     priors + clarify, and git history makes this reversible. → Delete it 
     (Recommended)

     edit
     Cut Preemptive Research; Build Plan Adaptation

     Context

     The CEO review (2026-08-02, SCOPE REDUCTION) cut preemptive research from
     the default
     intake path on measured evidence: 5 live runs, 2 rejected outright by the
     candidate, one
     confident miss against a firsthand HackerRank preview, and 3 of the last 8
     commits
     existing only to defend against the feature's own output. Every proven win
     came from
     interpreting user-supplied primary material (OA titles → "optimization
     themed"; Erik's
     notes → two correct drafts), never from web retrieval.

     What replaces it is the step the user's manual Cowork workflow had that was
     never built:
     adapt the plan as you learn things — paste an invite email, problem titles,
     or a
     friend's message against an existing target and let the remaining plan
     re-shape.

     Decisions locked during the review (D1–D6) plus two architectural
     amendments verified
     against the code:

     - D1, revised by D9 Research is deleted, not demoted. The launch lens
     settled
     it: an opt-in button on a 40%-rejected feature is a support liability in a
     paid
     product, and the networkless student is already served by model priors +
     clarifying
     questions (evidence-hierarchy tier 4). Git history archives the hardened
     code
     (citation gate, normalizeCitation, balanced-brace parser); TODOS #20 closes
     now
     instead of September. Default intake is describe → clarify (~15s).
     - D2 Adapt re-shapes pending items freely; ready items get flagged with a
     rebuild offer; done/generating are untouchable.
     - D3 Every applied adapt appends a record to t.adaptations[]; the record
     explains
     the change on the timeline and enables crash detection.
     - D4 (amended) No persistent lock. D5's preview-then-apply means the model
     call
     writes nothing; the only race is inside apply (no model call), closed by
     re-load-and-drop-stale. The adapting disk flag is dropped.
     - D5 Preview then apply: the model computes a diff, the user approves it,
     only then
     does anything write. No ungated model writer.
     - D6 Append-only specs: adaptation never edits an existing RoundSpec; it
     adds new
     ones and re-points pending items. History stays true by construction; UI
     derives
     display from queue items, tolerating orphan specs.
     - Amendment 1 The clarifier can't reconcile (input is {description, 
     context, findings, answers} — no specs). Adapt gets its own prompt + tool
     schema =
     clarify's ROUND_FIELDS plus supersedes: spec_id | null per round.
     draftToSpec,
     per-draft leniency, and validateRoundSpec are reused as-is.
     - Amendment 2 Apply writes target.json first (new specs + log entry), then
     queue.json. The intermediate state (orphan specs) is already legal.
     Recovery: on load,
     if the last adaptation record lists a re-point the queue doesn't reflect,
     re-apply it
     from the record.

     Phase A — delete research (ships first)

     Delete outright: server/src/research.ts, server/src/research.test.ts,
     prompts/research-round.md. Git history is the archive — the commit message
     records
     the evidence (five runs, two rejections, the Palantir miss) so restoring is
     a
     deliberate revert, not a rediscovery.

     server/src/app.ts
     - Remove the /api/research and /api/research/confirm handlers (~L585-617).
     - /api/clarify: drop the findings assembly (t.research?.confirmed ? … : '',
     L560-562); pass no findings.
     - /api/accept-spec: drop the t.research?.confirmed line from the
     topic-naming
     brief (~L650).
     - appPage(): reword the closing copy (~L445, "We'll look up what this round
     actually
     is…") to the paste-what-you-have promise.

     server/src/intake.ts — remove the Target.research field. Existing on-disk
     targets with a research key still load (bare JSON.parse ignores extras).

     server/src/clarify.ts + prompts/clarify-intake.md — remove the findings
     input field and {{FINDINGS}} placeholder. In the evidence hierarchy, drop
     tier 3
     (research findings) and renumber; keep tiers 1, 2, and model priors — the
     hierarchy
     still governs how pasted material outweighs priors, which is the part that
     earns its
     keep. Update clarify.test.ts fixtures accordingly.

     server/src/client/app.js
     - e-build handler: after POST /api/target succeeds, call runClarify(null)
     (today it calls runResearch(), app.js:127). Delete runResearch() and the
     findings-wall/skip/confirm rendering.
     - Reword the questions-screen meta line (app.js:196, "The research raised
     something
     worth settling…") — questions now come from the description/context alone.
     - Rewrite the stale header comment at app.js:7 (research (cited, 
     confirm-gated) → …).

     server/src/cli.ts — remove the research command/wiring (it imports
     research.ts;
     confirm nothing else does via grep -rn "research" server/ shared/ prompts/
     — the
     sweep must come back empty outside comments).

     TODOS.md — mark #20 resolved now ("closed early by D9, launch-lens revision
     of
     D1") rather than waiting for September.

     Phase B — the adapt core (pure, tested)

     New server/src/adapt.ts — pure functions, no I/O, no clock reads (take
     now),
     mirroring queue.ts conventions:

     interface AdaptDraft extends SpecDraft { supersedes: string | null }

     interface AdaptDiff {
       new_specs: RoundSpec[];               // append-only — never contains an 
     existing id
       repointed: { item_id: string; from_spec_id: string; to_spec_id: string;
                    old_title: string; new_title?: string }[];
       flagged: string[];                    // ready items built under a 
     superseded spec
       skipped: { item_id: string; reason: string }[];  // filled at apply time
       summary: string;                      // one sentence for the log + 
     timeline line
     }

     interface AdaptRecord {                 // Target.adaptations[] entry
       at: string; material_excerpt: string; // ≤280 chars
       summary: string; new_spec_ids: string[];
       repointed: { item_id: string; from: string; to: string }[];
       flagged: string[]; skipped: string[];
     }

     - gateAdapt(raw, existingSpecs) — mechanical gate in the gateClarify style:
     coerce
     rounds array, run each through draftToSpec with per-draft leniency (one bad
     draft
     never sinks siblings), validate supersedes references an existing spec id
     or null,
     reject a draft whose own id collides with an existing spec id
     (append-only), require
     ≥1 surviving draft.
     - planAdaptation(target, queue, drafts, now) → AdaptDiff — mechanical:
     active spec set = (existing specs not superseded) + new specs; re-point
     pending
     items round-robin across it (same arithmetic as proposeQueue,
     queue.ts:89-96),
     regenerating label; flag ready items whose spec is superseded; never touch
     done/generating/skipped. failed re-points like pending — it has no
     validated
     problem.
     - applyAdaptation(target, queue, diff, materialExcerpt, now) → { target, 
     queue } —
     returns new objects (deep-copy style like reconcileWithDisk), appends the
     AdaptRecord, sets stale: true on flagged items, drops (and records in
     skipped)
     any re-point whose item is no longer pending/failed in the queue passed in
     —
     the caller passes the freshly re-loaded queue.
     - reconcileAdaptation(target, queue) → Queue | null — the D3 recovery rule:
     if the
     last AdaptRecord lists a re-point the queue doesn't reflect, re-apply from
     the
     record; null when consistent. Called from refreshQueue alongside
     reconcileWithDisk.

     server/src/queue.ts — QueueItem gains stale?: boolean (optional; the bare
     JSON.parse loaders accept it, no migration).

     server/src/intake.ts — Target gains adaptations?: AdaptRecord[]. Extend the
     header diagram (intake.ts:4-10) with the second inbound path to target.json
     specs[].

     New prompts/adapt-plan.md — modeled on clarify-intake.md: the same
     capability
     vocabulary section, the same evidence hierarchy (firsthand beats
     everything; the pasted
     material IS firsthand), then: the target's CURRENT specs (id, label, shape
     line,
     emphasis), the new material, and emission rules — one round per distinct
     shape the
     material implies; set supersedes when a new shape replaces an existing
     spec's slot,
     null when it's additional; when the material doesn't contradict a spec,
     don't emit a
     replacement for it. No questions in v1 — the preview screen is the check.

     Model plumbing in adapt.ts — apiAdapter/claudePAdapter/pickAdapter
     mirroring clarify.ts:191-244 exactly (forced tool call; tool schema =
     ROUND_FIELDS + supersedes: {type: ['string','null']}; claude -p JSON
     fallback).

     Phase C — endpoints (server/src/app.ts)

     - POST /api/adapt {target_id, material} → 404 no target, 400 empty material
     or
     ▎ 256KB (loud), 400 no queue yet ("build a plan first"). Calls the adapter 
     →
     ▎ gateAdapt → planAdaptation, then names re-pointed items via the existing
     ▎ pickTopicNamer (one call per affected spec, failure degrades to unchanged
     titles —
     ▎ the quiet-rows rule) so the preview shows old → new names. Returns
     ▎ {diff, drafts} and writes nothing.
     - POST /api/adapt/apply {target_id, diff, material_excerpt} → server-side
     re-proof (house rule, same as accept-spec): every new_specs entry through
     validateRoundSpec; every supersedes/to_spec_id must resolve. Re-load target
     and queue fresh, call applyAdaptation, write target.json then queue.json,
     return
     the applied AdaptRecord (including what got skipped).
     - POST /api/rebuild {target_id, item_id} → item must be ready + stale:
     wipe the problem dir, clear stale, status → generating, spawnGeneration
     with the
     item's (new) spec and title — the retry path (app.ts:681-696) generalized.
     - /api/state additions: per-target adaptation: {at, summary} | null (latest
     record); stale flows through automatically via the item spread.
     - Fallback path: adapter/gate failure → 502 with the reason; material is in
     the request,
     nothing persisted, retry is safe.

     Phase D — client (server/src/client/app.js + CSS in appPage())

     - Timeline page: an add what you learned link under the paceline → inline
     panel
     (textarea + submit + cancel). Submit disables in-flight (no double-post),
     POST
     /api/adapt, then render the diff preview in the same panel:
     summary line · new rounds (shape lines, reusing specShapeLine) · re-pointed
     items as
     old title → new title · flagged ready items ("built for the old shape —
     will be
     offered a rebuild") · apply / discard. Apply → /api/adapt/apply →
     refresh(true);
     response's skipped[] non-empty → banner explaining what didn't change and
     why.
     Zero re-points and zero new specs at preview → say "nothing to change — the
     plan
     already matches" instead of an empty diff.
     - renderSeason: item with stale gets a metaline "built for the old round
     shape" +
     rebuild button (wired in wireTimeline like retry, → /api/rebuild).
     - Latest adaptation renders as one line under the paceline: Aug 5 — 
     {summary}.
     - The adapt textarea is covered by the existing userIsTyping() poll guard
     (app.js:434).

     Tests

     New server/src/adapt.test.ts — house style of queue.test.ts (frozen NOW,
     builder helpers, one behavior per it):
     - gate: supersedes → unknown id throws; new-draft id colliding with
     existing spec throws
     (append-only); one incoherent draft dropped, siblings survive.
     - plan: pending re-pointed round-robin; ready-under-superseded-spec flagged
     not moved;
     done/generating byte-identical before/after; failed re-points like pending.
     - apply: item that moved pending→generating between plan and apply lands in
     skipped;
     original spec objects never mutated (deep-equal snapshot); excerpt
     truncated at 280.
     - reconcile: record says item-3 → spec-B but queue says spec-A →
     re-applied; consistent
     state → null.
     - The 2am test: adapt while one item generates → that item untouched, skip
     recorded.

     server/src/app.test.ts — markup/source assertions in the existing style:
     negative assertions pinning the deletion (js contains no runResearch, html
     contains no research copy); adapt panel + button.rebuild wiring present;
     stale
     metaline string present.

     Reused (nothing re-invented)

     draftToSpec + validateRoundSpec (intake.ts:163), per-draft leniency pattern
     (clarify.ts:158-170), pickTopicNamer + quiet-rows degradation
     (app.ts:641-656),
     spawnGeneration (app.ts:81), retry semantics (app.ts:681),
     reconcileWithDisk
     call-site in refreshQueue, specShapeLine/wireTimeline/userIsTyping client
     helpers, the loud-400 validation style of /api/target.

     Verification

     1. npx vitest run — full suite green (251 existing + new adapt/app tests).
     2. Live on :3300 against the existing Palantir target:
       - New-plan flow: describe → clarify questions arrive in ~15s with no 
     research
     step anywhere; an old target that still has a research key on disk loads
     and
     renders fine.
       - Adapt drill: add what you learned → paste Erik's notes → preview shows
     re-pointed
     titles and any flagged items → apply → timeline shows the adaptation line;
     the
     re-pointed rounds carry new titles; done rows unchanged.
       - Race drill: start a generation, immediately submit an adapt including
     that item →
     apply reports it skipped; the generation completes normally to ready.
       - Rebuild drill: flag an item via an adapt that supersedes its spec →
     rebuild →
     status walks generating → ready with the new shape.
     3. Crash drill (manual): hand-edit queue.json to revert one re-point after
     an applied
     adapt, restart, confirm reconcileAdaptation re-applies it from the record.

          +"paste it raw — an invite email, problem titles from the asses

      579 +async function previewAdapt(tid) {                            
      580 +  if (!adapt || adapt.phase === 'busy') return; // in-flight g
          +uard                                                          
      581 +  adapt.phase = 'busy';                                       
      582 +  rerender();                                                 
      583 +  const r = await fetch('/api/adapt', { method: 'POST', header
          +s: { 'content-type': 'application/json' }, body: JSON.stringif
          +y({ target_id: tid, material: adapt.material || '' }) });     
      584 +  const d = await r.json();                                   
      585 +  if (!adapt || adapt.tid !== tid) return; // cancelled meanwh
          +ile                                                           
      586 +  if (d.error) { adapt.phase = 'error'; adapt.error = d.error;
          + } else { adapt.phase = 'preview'; adapt.result = d; }        
      587 +  rerender();                                                 
      588 +}                                                             
      589 +                                                              
      590 +async function applyAdapt(tid) {                              
      591 +  if (!adapt || adapt.phase === 'busy') return;               
      592 +  const diff = adapt.result.diff;                             
      593 +  const excerpt = (adapt.material || '').slice(0, 280);       
      594 +  adapt.phase = 'busy';                                       
      595 +  rerender();                                                 
      596 +  const r = await fetch('/api/adapt/apply', { method: 'POST', 
          +headers: { 'content-type': 'application/json' }, body: JSON.st
          +ringify({ target_id: tid, diff, material_excerpt: excerpt }) }
          +);                                                            
      597 +  const d = await r.json();                                   
      598 +  if (d.error) {                                              
      599 +    if (adapt && adapt.tid === tid) { adapt.phase = 'error'; a
          +dapt.error = d.error; rerender(); }                           
      600 +    return;                                                   
      601 +  }                                                           
      602 +  if (d.record && d.record.skipped.length) {                  
      603 +    el('banner').innerHTML = '<div class="banner">' + d.record
          +.skipped.length +                                             
      604 +      ' round(s) didn\'t change — they moved on while you were
          + deciding (' +                                                
      605 +      esc(d.record.skipped.map((s) => s.reason).join(', ')) + 
          +')</div>';                                                    
      606 +  }                                                           
      607 +  adapt = null;                                               
      608 +  refresh(true);                                              
      609 +}                                                             
      610 +                                                              
      611  // ---- entrance choreography: staggered rise + count-up, once
            per page
      612  //      visit, never replayed by the 5s poll; off under reduce
           d motion ----

     ELI10: Your generator already covers six round archetypes in the IDE. The

     generate, so the half of real prep that lives on LeetCode is invisible to

❯ Before we talk about implementation, I want to understand what we currently   
  support and the scope of what people can practice, how it supports different  
  formats currently                                                             

⏺ Good — let's stay on understanding. Here's the full picture of what the
  product supports today, from the candidate's seat.

  The mental model: one canvas, formats as combinations

  Zenkai has exactly one canvas: a real VS Code editor in the browser (Docker
  openvscode), with a file tree, a terminal, a test runner, and an optional
  voice interviewer. There is no format menu anywhere in the code. Instead,
  every round is described by five switches (shared/src/round-spec.ts), and each
  switch changes something concrete about the session:

  ┌─────────────────┬───────────────────────────────────────────────────────┐
  │     Switch      │        What it actually changes in the session        │
  ├─────────────────┼───────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │                 │ true: the voice interviewer is live — it probes,      │
  │ interviewer:    │ answers clarifying questions, interrupts silence.     │
  │ true/false      │ false (OA mode): nobody replies, but the mic stays    │
  │                 │ on, because thinking aloud is still judge signal.     │
  ├─────────────────┼───────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │                 │ true: a Run Tests affordance exists and works. false: │
  │ can_run_tests   │  it's absent entirely — for rounds where executing    │
  │                 │ isn't allowed.                                        │
  ├─────────────────┼───────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │ time_limit_ms   │ set: countdown in the chrome, session auto-finalizes  │
  │                 │ at zero. null: untimed.                               │
  ├─────────────────┼───────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │                 │ repo: you open into an existing codebase to           │
  │ starts_from     │ read/fix/extend. blank: a scaffold you build from.    │
  │                 │ diff: someone's change you review.                    │
  ├─────────────────┼───────────────────────────────────────────────────────┤
  │                 │ iterate: run tests freely as a tool. one_shot: graded │
  │ submit          │  once server-side at submit — the suite is not an     │
  │                 │ iteration tool, just like a real autograder.          │
  └─────────────────┴───────────────────────────────────────────────────────┘

  Plus one non-negotiable per problem: the check kind — the mechanical proof
  that a generated problem is real. The generator never grades its own homework;
  the validator proves one_failing_test (there's exactly one planted failure),
  all_failing (a visible suite you build against), all_passing (a green repo you
  extend without breaking), or diff_present (the review artifact exists). A
  problem that fails its proof never reaches you.

  The six archetypes this yields — and the real rounds they cover

  1. Debugging round — repo + one failing test. "Here's a codebase, something's
  wrong, find it." This is the most exercised path: your Python ledger session,
  the learning-round sessions. Covers Palantir-style learning rounds, "debug our
  service" screens.
  2. Build-to-suite OA — blank + all_failing + timed + one_shot. Visible failing
  tests, implement until green, graded once. This is the HackerRank/CodeSignal
  species — your Palantir OA drills this week ran exactly this shape.
  3. Extend/refactor round — repo + all_passing. Green codebase, add a
  capability without breaking it. Covers "add a feature to our API" onsites.
  Generatable today, lightly exercised.
  4. Code review round — diff + diff_present, tests optional. "Review this PR."
  In-vocabulary, least exercised of the four.
  5. Mock interview — any of the above with interviewer: true. The voice
  interviewer runs the room; the judge grades clarify/communicate against a live
  conversation.
  6. Silent OA — the same problems with interviewer: false. Same judge, but
  expectations re-shape: clarify becomes "pin down the spec from the visible
  tests," verify becomes "dry-run before your one submit."

  So OA vs mock interview isn't two features — it's one bit, and the generator
  writes different per-dimension expectations depending on that bit (that's
  explicit in generate-round.md: expectations are written "for the round's
  shape").

  Languages: TypeScript (vitest) and Python (unittest, stdlib-only). That's the
  whole runtime story today.

  What every round gets regardless of shape: generated from your pasted
  reference material ("same species, never a copy"), mechanically validated
  before it's offered, judged across the six dimensions (clarify, approach, 
  communicate, implement, verify, reflect) with problem-specific expectations,
  and fed into the gap graph that re-aims future generation.

  What's outside the envelope, and why

  Two different kinds of "no":

  Canvas-bound (we don't have the surface):
  - System design / whiteboard — needs a drawing canvas, not an editor.
  - SQL rounds — no SQL runtime; the two runtimes are TS and Python.
  - Frontend/UI rounds — no browser preview canvas to render against.
  - Behavioral / pure conversation — no code artifact for the canvas at all.

  These are what the intake's unsupported clause declines honestly at plan time
  — "the product declines rather than faking it" is a design rule.

  Corpus-bound (we have the canvas, the incumbent has the corpus):
  - LeetCode-style DS&A is technically inside the envelope — blank + all_failing
  + timed + one_shot is a LeetCode problem, and we can generate them. What we
  can't replicate is what makes LeetCode good at it: difficulty calibrated by
  millions of submissions, known-fair test coverage, community solutions to
  compare against. This is why "just link out for LeetCode drills" is a live
  question rather than an obvious no — it's the one format where generating is
  possible but arguably worse than the incumbent.

  Not built yet, in neither bucket: drills (TODO #13) — the 2-5 minute reps
  between full rounds. The queue, generator, and judge are all rounds-sized
  today; drills need their own item size, a judge-lite grading path, and a
  surface. Deferred by explicit decision on 2026-07-31 (rounds-only queue ships
  first, P2 after the queue proves itself).

  The one-sentence version

  Anything that is code in an editor with a provable pass criterion — debugging,
  build-to-suite, extend, review, timed or not, autograded or live-interviewed,
  in TS or Python — we generate, validate, run, and judge natively today;
  anything needing a different canvas (whiteboard, SQL pad, browser) we decline
  honestly; and LeetCode-style DS&A sits in the awkward middle where we can
  generate it but the incumbent's corpus is better than anything we'd make.

  That middle category, plus drills, is where the native-vs-link-out scoping
  question actually lives. Happy to pick the review back up whenever you want —
  or dig further into any of these first.

✻ Brewed for 41s

❯ I think maybe we can have linking to LC as an option? Ie if people are        
  practicing for a big tech OA w/ no interviewer and its purely LC, then it can 
  be an option for the plan generator to just link the relevant lc question in  
  (or just include the title if links are too expensive). But if its a          
  question of running tests w/ an interviewer, then maybe we somehow paste the  
  lc question w/ some tests into our editor here. What do you think? I think    
  interviewing is in a weird space where LC can still be relevant but its no    
  longer the predominant format anymroe                                         

⏺ Your split is right, and it maps cleanly onto machinery that already exists.
  Let me sharpen it and flag one trap.

  The no-interviewer pure-LC case: link/title out. Agreed, and this is where
  linking earns its place. If the target is an Amazon-style OA that's genuinely
  two LC mediums, the honest plan row is "do these on LeetCode," not a
  home-grown imitation with uncalibrated difficulty. On links vs titles: titles 
  are the reliable primitive, links are best-effort decoration. The model knows
  the famous corpus's titles cold, but URLs are guessable slugs — mostly right
  for famous problems, confidently wrong at the tail, and a plan with a dead
  link feels broken in a way a searchable title never does. Same lesson as the
  research cut: don't emit what you can't verify. "Two Sum, Course Schedule II —
  leetcode.com" is robust; a hallucinated slug isn't.

  The interviewer case: you don't actually want to paste LC verbatim — and you 
  already have something better. Here's the trap: pasting a real LC question +
  tests into our editor has two problems. Legally, LC problem statements are
  copyrighted content we'd be redistributing inside a paid product. Practically,
  a famous problem is a bad mock — if you've seen Course Schedule before, the
  interviewer is watching you recite, and the judge's approach verdict is
  garbage. But notice the reference-material path already does the legitimate
  version of exactly what you're describing: paste the LC problem as context, 
  and the generator produces a same-species problem — same difficulty, same
  algorithmic core, different skin — with a test suite our validator actually
  proves. That's not a workaround, it's strictly better for a mock: the
  conversation is real because the problem is unseen, and the judge grades
  genuine clarify/approach behavior instead of recall. This shipped weeks ago;
  it's the "never a copy" rule in generate-round.md:19-22.

  And the middle case you didn't name — live LC-style with an interviewer but no
  test running (Google/Meta phone-screen style, CoderPad, eyeball-your-code) —
  the capability space already expresses: interviewer: true, can_run_tests: 
  false, starts_from: blank. That's a supported combination today, not a new
  format.

  So the whole design compresses to one scheduling rule the plan generator
  follows:

  Is the round autograded-LC (no interviewer, famous corpus)?
    → external item: LC titles (+ best-effort link), self-checked done
  Everything else (any interviewer, or any non-LC shape)?
    → native generation, seeded by whatever the candidate pastes (incl. LC
  problems)

  On your last point — you're right, and it's the founding premise of this
  product: LC still gatekeeps the OA layer at big tech, but the onsite layer has
  visibly moved toward debugging, realistic codebases, and AI-era practical
  rounds. Which means the external-item path is a bridge for one layer of the 
  funnel, not the product. The plan schedules LC reps where LC genuinely rules;
  everything differentiated stays native.

  That folds your proposal directly into the approach question — reformulated:

     now and a whole new generator/grading path ships without its own review.

⏺ User answered Claude's questions:
  ⎿  · T1 — Cross-model tension: who supplies LC titles?

     The review's design: model emits titles marked well_known, gate drops 
     unattested. Codex: self-attestation is still a model claim shipping false 
     titles with product authority; schedule user-provided titles verbatim + 
     topic fallback rows, and don't invent problems.

     ELI10: When the plan says "do Two Sum and Course Schedule II", those names 
     either came from you (a list you pasted, always real) or from the model's 
     memory (usually real, occasionally invented). The question is whether the 
     model gets to name problems at all.

     RECOMMENDATION: A — it's your own research lesson applied consistently: 
     user-supplied material schedules verbatim (tier-1 evidence), the model only
     ever emits TOPIC rows ("3 graph mediums"), which carry the guidance value 
     with zero invented-title risk. Completeness: A=9/10, B=7/10, C=8/10. → 
     Keep: attested model titles
     · T2 — Cross-model tension: how deep does the debrief→gap bridge go now?

     The review descoped E1 to "append note with source discriminator, half 
     weight read-side." Codex: that's not a design — which gap key does a note 
     map to, what polarity, how does it hit focus/recency? Also caught: 
     un-done/re-done duplicates evidence under append-only.

     ELI10: You write "the DP one wrecked me" after checking off reps. For that 
     to steer your plan, something must decide it's negative evidence about a 
     specific weakness. That's either a real design (a small model call mapping 
     note→dimension) or storage-only for now.

     RECOMMENDATION: B — store debriefs on the item (one slot, overwrite on 
     re-done — fixing the duplication bug) and DEFER graph ingestion to the 
     drills build, where judge-lite gives self-reported evidence a principled 
     home. Half-designed evidence channels into the thing that aims your 
     practice is how subtle mis-aiming starts. Note: options differ in kind, not
     coverage — no completeness score. → Defer ingestion, store only 
     (Recommended)
